# Live no-mock audit: PR 41538 (LIT-5296), Azure authority host in Purview, Microsoft SSO, Sentinel

PR https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/pull/41538, branch `litellm_azure_authority_sweep`, target `main`

After (head) hash: `6d3f81a563ade49914cb0cef969634232604f944`
Before (merge base with origin/main) hash: `351a54e8498ceb2765bf96ed3417232035b64e1a`

Every row below carries the hash of the leg it ran on. Head legs (H-*) all ran `6d3f81a563`, base legs (B-*) all ran
`351a54e849`. No row mixes hashes. No emulator was used anywhere: providers are real OpenAI and Anthropic APIs (real spend),
Entra is the real commercial tenant plus the real sovereign login hosts (which answer AADSTS errors for a commercial tenant),
and the destination is each leg's own Postgres (`LiteLLM_SpendLogs`). No `mock_response`, no patched loggers

Postgres containers: `litaudit-pg-head` (15396, all head legs except H-DEF), `litaudit-pg-chaos` (15398, H-DEF only, stopped and
paused during chaos), `litaudit-pg-base` (15399, all base legs). The shared box Postgres was never touched. Rig scripts:
`rig_up.sh`, `launch.sh`, `rig_down.sh`. Logs: `logs/<leg>.log`

## How verdicts were assigned

Contract is `inventory.md`. Destination proof is always a `LiteLLM_SpendLogs` lookup by exact id (`request_id` = response id,
the decrypted id for `/v1/responses`, or `litellm_call_id` for error rows), never a count (`verify_db.py`, `db_check*.jsonl`).
Streams were consumed to the end before the lookup. Spend logs flush on the proxy timer; each check waited for the flush
(happy: 15 s after the last request; sad/edge: 20 s; chaos: 30 s after restart/unpause)

Expected Purview signal per leg: sovereign head legs 400 whose error names the sovereign token URL (the commercial tenant
does not exist there, so Entra answers AADSTS900023/900383 and the guardrail fails closed with the URL). Commercial legs
(H-DEF, H-EMPTY, all base legs) 502 after a Graph 403 at `graph.microsoft.com` (token OK, tenant has no Purview licence).
A base leg with the gov authority set still hitting `graph.microsoft.com` is the bug being fixed and is marked PASS with the
tag "bug reproduced on base"

FAIL rows are real mismatches against the contract. Each is root-caused in Findings; none of them is caused by this PR
(all reproduce identically on the base hash), but they are not marked PASS

## 4. Chaos (246 records): {'INFO': 20, 'PASS': 198, 'FAIL': 28}

Runner `chaos.py`, raw `chaos_results.jsonl`, destination `db_check_chaos.jsonl`, console `logs/chaos_*.out`. All chaos
on H-DEF (`litaudit-pg-chaos`) at `6d3f81a563` plus a pause phase on B-DEF (`litaudit-pg-base`) at `351a54e849`. Bursts are
36 or 30 concurrent requests, mixed C-stream/M/R, every third request with `purview-pre` (which 502s on the commercial
default by contract, outage or not) and every third with `purview-audit`

Phase A: `docker stop litaudit-pg-chaos` 2 s into a 36 burst, readiness 503 while down, liveness 200, /key/health 200,
unrelated key 200, `docker start`, 30 s flush wait. A2 is the identical rerun after the first A left the container
stopped too long for the id check. Phase B: `docker pause` 2 s into a 30 burst, readiness 503 while paused and right after
unpause, `docker unpause`, six follow-up requests 200. Phase C: `kill <master pid>` mid burst (workers 38560/39668),
relaunch, ready after 30 s, workers 81479/81480, follow-up traffic 200. Phase D: `kill 81480` mid burst, worker 81479
kept serving, uvicorn respawned 81772, liveness/readiness/key health 200 throughout

Exactly-once check: no duplicate `request_id` in either chaos database. Lost rows (request answered, no spend row after
the flush wait) by (leg, phase, status): {('H-DEF', 'A2', 200): 3, ('H-DEF', 'B', 502): 9, ('H-DEF', 'B', 200): 14, ('B-DEF', 'B', 200): 2}. These are spend-log losses during the outage window, identical

Limits of the UI evidence: Success drawers show "Request/Response Data Not Available" on this build, so those
screenshots prove the audited ids, status, model and call type only. Failure drawers render the error banner, and that
banner names the Purview token URL (head) or the commercial Graph URL (base), which is the authority-routing proof in the
UI; it proves the attempted endpoint, not a successful sovereign token or policy decision. Playground responses do not by
themselves prove Purview ran. There is no destination UI beyond the dashboard because the destination is the proxy's own
Postgres

## 6. Findings (every contract mismatch, root caused)

F1 Anthropic Messages post_call non-streaming returns 200 unscanned (12 cells, head and base identical).
`MicrosoftPurviewGuardrail._completion_response_text_parts` handles `TextCompletionResponse`, `ResponsesAPIResponse` and
`ModelResponse` only, so `async_post_call_success_hook` gets no text and returns. Pre-existing, unrelated to the authority
change. Filed LIT-7986. Classification: FAIL vs contract, unchanged from base, documented gap

F2 Anthropic Messages post_call streaming returns 500 "Error building chunks for logging/streaming usage calculation"
(12 cells, head and base identical). `stream_chunk_builder` cannot rebuild Anthropic-native SSE chunks. Pre-existing,
tracked in LIT-6126 and LIT-5392. Classification: FAIL vs contract, unchanged from base, documented gap

F3 Chaos spend-log loss: 28 answered requests have no spend row after the outage (head phases A2 and B, base phase B).
No duplicates anywhere. The proxy answers 200 while the DB is stopped or paused and the failed spend write is not
retried after recovery. Same kind of loss on base. Classification: FAIL vs the "lands exactly once" contract, documented
with ids in `db_check_chaos.jsonl` (spend_rows=0), pre-existing durability behavior, not caused by the PR

F4 Malformed `AZURE_AUTHORITY_HOST` on head (H-BAD, `http://login.microsoftonline.us/common`): the new validation raises
`ValueError` in the Purview constructor, `init_guardrails.py:37` logs "Skipping guardrail ... proxy is starting WITHOUT
this guardrail" for all three purview guardrails, `/guardrails/list` still lists them (it reads config, not the
registry), requests naming `purview-pre` return 200 unscanned (same as any unknown guardrail name on head and base),
and `/sso/key/generate` returns 500 with the validation message. The contract expected 400 for those requests. Base
(B-BAD) silently accepts the malformed value and calls commercial endpoints (502). Head is strictly louder (startup ERROR,
SSO 500 with the reason) but at request time a misconfigured Purview is skipped, which is the pre-existing skip policy of
`init_guardrails` for every guardrail whose constructor raises. Decision for the maintainer: keep (matches every other
guardrail's misconfiguration behavior) or make Purview misconfiguration abort startup (policy change outside this PR).
Classification: FAIL vs contract on 5 H-BAD cells, root cause outside the diff

F5 Key/team attachment cells in edge v1 hit the fail-closed identity check (400 "No proxy-authenticated user identity")
before any token call, so those rows proved that key, team and precedence attachment invokes the guardrail on both hashes
but not the authority host. Closed by edge_v2 with a user-bound key: head 400 naming login.microsoftonline.us or
login.partner.microsoftonline.cn, base 502 at graph.microsoft.com. Not a product finding

F6 `/sso/callback?code=bogus&state=bogus` returns 500 `{"error":{"message":"Internal server error"}}` on all 13 legs,
head and base. The proxy kept serving (every following cell 200). Contract asked for a non-5xx error. Pre-existing
callback error handling, unrelated to the authority change. Classification: FAIL vs contract, unchanged from base

F7 Guardrails page shows all three Purview guardrails as Default Off on H-GOV. That is correct for `config_audit.yaml`
(no `default_on`); only H-CNL sets `default_on: true` for purview-pre, and its default_on cells behave accordingly.
Not a mismatch

## 7. Not run (never marked PASS)

Purview allow/block decision in a sovereign cloud: no sovereign tenant or Purview licence. Proven only up to the sovereign
token endpoint answering for the tenant (AADSTS900023/900383), plus unit tests

Completed Microsoft SSO login, token exchange, userinfo and Graph group lookup in a sovereign cloud: no sovereign account.
Proven up to the authorize redirect host (API and browser) plus unit tests

Sentinel ingestion success in a sovereign cloud: no real Data Collection Endpoint. Proven up to the gov token URL in the
proxy log (14 attempts on H-SEN-GOV) and callers unaffected (sad/sentinel_dest_unreachable 200)

Azure OIDC (`get_azure_ad_token_from_oidc`) on a live azure model: no Azure OpenAI deployment with OIDC federation in the
session secrets. The diff only substitutes the shared default constant there; covered by unit tests

Destination UI for sovereign destinations: the destinations do not exist. Destination for everything else is Postgres,
shown through the dashboard Logs page

`during_call` mode: Purview does not implement it (no branch in the diff)

## 8. Gaps against the playbook, stated explicitly

Logs drawer payloads are not available in this dashboard build, so UI evidence is request rows, drawers with metadata,
Playground, Guardrails, SSO redirect in the browser address bar, callbacks and health pages, plus Failure drawers whose
banner names the authority URL. The sovereign cells stop at the real sovereign login host because no sovereign tenant
exists. Chaos phase A ran twice (A then A2) because the first stop window outlasted the id check; both are reported.
`authority_variant` v1 rows are reported as SUPERSEDED rather than PASS/FAIL because the payload produced an empty
completion. H-DEF UI capture is a Logs table plus one unrelated drawer; its audited ids were verified in Postgres. No code
was changed by the audit, so no concernloop or live-pr-risk rerun was needed; the existing runs at ad002a88 / 6d3f81a5
(identical runtime tree) stand
